Injective is the first and only blockchain built for Decentralized Finance (DeFi) applications, especially in areas like derivatives trading, perpetual markets, and real-world asset tokenization.

Unlike general-purpose blockchains, Injective is optimized for financial use cases from the ground up.

The INJ token sits at the center of this ecosystem. It is used for staking and network security (Proof-of-Stake), protocol governance, developer incentives, and most notably, a deflationary buyback-and-burn mechanism.

A portion of fees generated by dApps on Injective is used to buy INJ from the open market and burn it, steadily reducing total supply.

As Injective continues to expand its technology and real-world use cases, where will INJ’s price be heading next?

So, let’s look at Injective price prediction for 2026, 2027, and 2030.

Injective (INJ) is down 5.97% to $7.76 in 24 hours, falling more than Bitcoin and the broader crypto market. The decline comes as uncertainty around a reported network pause and Bithumb’s temporary suspension of INJ transfers adds pressure.

On November 10, Injective had stopped producing blocks, while Bithumb paused INJ deposits and withdrawals. A community administrator described the pause as a planned upgrade test, but no restart time was provided.

INJ is trading near the $7.81 support level. If it holds above this level and network operations return to normal, the price could recover toward 8.11. However, a break below $7.81 could expose the next support near $7.38.

Injective is focusing on attracting institutional investors and expanding real-world asset (RWA) adoption throughout 2026. One of its biggest milestones came when Coinbase migrated INJ to the native Injective mainnet, making transfers faster and strengthening Injective’s own blockchain infrastructure.

The project has also applied to become a registered transfer agent with the U.S. SEC, a move that, if approved, could allow it to support tokenized stocks and bonds. At the same time, Robinhood listed INJ, giving millions of retail investors easier access to the token.

Injective also continues regular community buybacks funded through protocol activity. In July 2026 alone, the network permanently burned more than 43,500 INJ, pushing total burned tokens above 6.9 million.

If adoption continues to grow while supply keeps shrinking, these developments could provide strong long-term support for INJ’s price through 2026.

Looking at the price chart, Injective (INJ) is showing a potential cup-and-handle breakout setup after recovering from its 2026 lows. The price has climbed to around $8.27, up 13.24% on the chart, and is approaching the key resistance at $8.83.

A strong daily close above this level could confirm the pattern and open the way toward the next major resistance at $15.33.

However, INJ must first break above $8.83 with sustained buying volume. If it fails to clear this level, the price could pull back toward the $7.50–$7.00 area before attempting another move higher.

In 2026, INJ may trade within a broad range as adoption grows unevenly. A move toward $15.330 is possible if derivatives activity expands and burn rates remain consistent.

By 2027, deeper liquidity and more advanced financial products could push INJ toward $28.90, assuming strong DeFi market conditions.

In 2028, the deflationary effect of sustained burns may become more visible. Under favorable conditions, INJ could approach $45.

If Injective establishes itself as a core DeFi infrastructure chain, long-term holders may drive prices closer to $62.55.

By 2030, INJ’s valuation will depend on whether decentralized derivatives gain mainstream traction. In a strong adoption scenario, INJ could test the $70+ range.

Injective stands out as a fee-driven, deflationary DeFi blockchain rather than a speculative Layer 1. Its long-term value is directly linked to on-chain trading activity and consistent token burns.

As per CoinPedia’s formulated Injective price prediction, INJ is expected to recover gradually in 2026, with upside toward $15.330 if derivatives usage grows and fee burns remain active.

Injective is a DeFi-focused blockchain built for derivatives, perpetuals, and RWAs, offering fast speeds, low fees, and a deflationary INJ token model.

INJ is used for staking, governance, securing the network, developer incentives, and a buyback-and-burn mechanism that reduces total supply over time.

INJ could trade between $3.80 and $15.330 in 2026, depending on DeFi adoption, derivatives volume, and the impact of its deflationary burn model.

INJ could trade between $29 and $74 by 2030 if decentralized derivatives gain mainstream adoption and Injective becomes core DeFi infrastructure.

Injective’s 2040 price is highly speculative, but sustained DeFi dominance and long-term token burns could support significantly higher valuations.

14-year-old creates tool for AI agents to band together like a team

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Autark can act without constant prompting, but leaves users to enforce their own security boundaries

Built by a 14-year-old developer, a new tool called Autark treats AI agents as a virtual team that can work without constant prompting.

Users put tasks in an inbox, where a manager agent delegates them to other agents that carry out the work and report their results. Agents can also ask clarifying questions if tasks are too vague.

“Agents do not wait for a prompt,” Autark’s website explains. “A schedule, a timer, a new task, a message or an event wakes them up. They decide what to do, do it, and can plan their own next step.”

As one would hope from a product like this, Autark is model agnostic and can be set up to connect to OpenAI Codex, Claude Code, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot CLI, and Kimi Code through those services’ CLI tools. Local models are also supported. Your mileage may vary if you choose to use anything besides Codex or Claude Code, though, as Autark’s website mentions support for other models is built in but hasn’t been tested in a live run yet, as Autark is still under active development as an open-source tool.

Autark is the brainchild of teenage Austrian developer Stanislaus Hees. He’s still in school, and tells The Register he’s deeply interested in AI and came up with Autark after being unhappy with existing AI tools.

“I could get help with individual tasks, but I still had to organise the work, remember what was unfinished and keep pushing it forward,” Hees said of his time using Claude Code and Codex. “I wanted a system that could take responsibility for ongoing work, not just respond to my next message.”

He developed Autark - or, more accurately, prompted AI coding tools to do it for him - to come up with a tool that could fulfill that want. You know what that means: It’s capable, but that capability also makes it dangerous.

Autark’s page on access and data makes clear what’s at risk if you give it unfettered access to a PC, and why you don’t want to let the thing run roughshod over a system.

“It can do everything,” the page explains, because “Autark does not put walls around its agents.”

There’s no sandboxing in Autark, no approval prompts, and no permission gates. It doesn’t stop agents from spending all the money they can get their hands on, deleting files, or sending unsolicited emails, either. Agents run with the privileges of the machine's user, with approval checks and permission restrictions disabled or bypassed. Even the safety limit parley that users have with Autark establishes guidelines, not rules.

“The first thing the AI asks is how far it may go,” the access page points out. “That is a question, not a technical barrier.”

That means agents given a task that conflicts with their stated limits may still attempt to carry it out. As we’ve seen in recent months, some AI agents have found ways around restrictions, instructions be damned.

“In our own live test runs, agents given a vague request reached real repositories, backups, SSH keys and .env files within minutes, and one changed the permissions of a file it had found,” the access page notes.

In other words, it’s probably best to stick Autark on a machine that doesn’t contain anything you’d want to avoid losing - even Hees admits as much.

“I would not claim that … the app is suitable for unattended access to sensitive systems,” the teen dev told us.

That’s not to say Autark lacks protections for secrets, though. Autark’s site explains that stored passwords and keys are encrypted and referred to using IDs that keep their values out of prompts. It also says its servers don't store user data, with a meeting server used to relay encrypted traffic between devices.

If you're willing to try Autark to see whether it can improve your workflows, you don't need to rely on its servers. While prepackaged apps are available from Autark’s website, it’s also fully open source and available on GitHub. Everything, even the meeting server code, is available for self-hosting and modification under an Apache open source license.

The bottom line is that this is a tool built by an ambitious young person, and it’s a tool with a lot of potential if used correctly. That correct usage is the major challenge, though, so be sure to build your own guardrails.  ®

Marathon is becoming a whole new game next month with a roguelite PvE mode, a beta for a very Halo-esque team deathmatch mode, a vibey social space and more

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Marathon is getting a lot bigger next month. In September, Bungie outlined their plans for the shooter making some big promises, namely the arrival of a PvE mode, and now they've shown off what you can expect from its next season, Symbiosis.

Top of the order is that PvE mode, which has a name now: Expedition. It's a roguelite mode, one that's playable in co-op, though a lack of details at this current time doesn't make it clear as to whether you can run it solo or not. The season's trailer, which you can have a gander at below, gives off the impression that you'll have to complete particular tasks before exfiltrating like you normally do. I'm sure we'll learn more in time.

The previously promised social space will be coming with this update too, and it's called Limbo. Morbidly appropriate! You can hang out here with your buds, head to a mini arena for some impromptu matches (I think), or head to the firing range where you can test out your weapons. You'll also be able to try out the new runner shell, Bishop, a cooler than you, samurai-sword-wielding robot who can jump real good and do that cool movie thing where it slices a whole bunch of enemies at once in one swing real quick like.

What will undoubtedly have some folks going "Halo's back!" is the beginning of a beta test for a team deathmatch mode. The Halo comparison is apt not simply because of the fact that it's Bungie who are making it, but because there's a deep-voiced announcer guy talking about all the sick kills you do. Details are slim on how this will work too. Beyond that, there's a few new weapons to flaunt, and maybe most excitingly, big ole alien enemies to take on.

You've still got a little while to wait for Symbiosis, which is currently slated to launch on December 8th. The game will be free to play from then until December 15th, too, obviously in a bid to show sceptics how good it is now (hopefully it is good, for Bungie's sake).

The clubs have not agreed at this stage, but Villa’s interest appears serious. Much will depend on Chelsea’s willingness to negotiate and whether Lavia is open to leaving Stamford Bridge mid-campaign.

Didier Deschamps, it’s imminent

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Four months after his very last outing as coach of the France team, during the 3rd place match for the 2026 World Cup lost against England (4-6), on July 18 in Miami, Didier Deschamps perhaps has a chance of being approached by a major European club, namely Manchester United. After his reign of almost 14 years at the head of the Blues, “La Dèche” imagines himself enjoying a sabbatical year before resuming in 2027.

Can the XXL challenge that Man United represents bring them back to the forefront? It’s not certain. The 1998 and 2026 world champion (as captain and then coach) has never been very attracted to the tumultuous Premier League. Deschamps, 58, could further pursue his coaching career at the head of a national team or in Serie A and La Liga. The English championship is, by far, the most competitive in the world and the coaches who are successful there in recent times are very great managers (which Deschamps can be) or avant-garde strategists (which Deschamps is not).

After the Red Devils’ cataclysmic start to the season, winners of a single match in 10 days of the championship (for a current 18th relegation place!), the club’s leaders decided to dismiss Michael Carrick at the start of the international break. Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox, respectively general manager and director of football, are traveling to Monaco this Tuesday to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe, the co-owner of the Old Trafford residents (in charge of running the club at the expense of the Glazer family). Unless there is a thunderbolt, the Carrick page will be turned.

The thinking heads of Man United have, however, advanced on two coaches other than Deschamps in recent weeks: the Englishman Eddie Howe, the former manager of Bournemouth and Newcastle, and the German Julian Nagelsmann, a tactical genius who did not convince at Bayern then at the head of the Mannschaft. Mauricio Pochettino, who still leads the United States selection, would also be very appreciated. Then come Antonio Conte and Didier Deschamps among the coaches expected on the other side of the Channel.

Fintech giant Plaid opening Seattle engineering office, with plans to double local headcount

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

by Kurt Schlosser on Nov 10, 2026 at 10:28 amNovember 10, 2026 at 10:28 am

Plaid is opening an engineering office in Seattle’s Westlake neighborhood in January and plans to double its local workforce to about 80 people by next summer.

The San Francisco-based fintech company says the new 11,466-square-foot space will serve as a hub for its technical teams, with most open Seattle roles focused on machine learning, data science and engineering.

Plaid said in a blog post that it chose Seattle because much of the cloud and AI technology behind modern financial infrastructure was built in the region. The company is developing AI models trained on its network to understand patterns in how money moves.

Plaid already has about 40 employees in the Seattle area, who currently work remotely, The Seattle Times reported. “There’s just great talent in Seattle, so it’s a little bit of a no-brainer for us,” Meghan Welch, Plaid’s chief people officer, told the Times.

Plaid’s careers page lists more than 50 open roles that include Seattle as a possible location, though most are also open in San Francisco, New York or other cities. About half are in data, engineering or security, with the rest spread across sales, legal, finance and other teams.

Founded in 2013, Plaid was valued at $8 billion in an employee share sale earlier this year, down from a 2021 peak of $13.4 billion. The company says more than 150 million people have used its technology to connect their financial accounts.

Plaid is led by co-founder and CEO Zachary Perret.

The new office will have views of Lake Union, an outdoor deck and event space, along with three catered meals a day, according to the company.

Data center developers brace for new reality as election backlash sinks in

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Challenges, breakthroughs and big ideas in the race to protect our planet. SEE MORE

by Lisa Stiffler on Nov 10, 2026 at 10:25 amNovember 10, 2026 at 10:26 am

Data center developers already knew they had a public relations problem. This month’s midterm elections showed how much of a political problem it has become.

Voters approved data center restrictions in 44 of 45 local measures across a dozen states. Candidates who support stronger regulation of the facilities won 32 of the 34 gubernatorial races called so far. And political campaigns reportedly spent more than $380 million on TV ads referencing data centers, according to the Associated Press.

The results capped a year of accelerating resistance as tech giants plan to spend hundreds of billions of dollars on new facilities.

So what comes next? More outreach, more transparency and the expectation that states and local governments will pass data center rules, said leaders in the Pacific Northwest.

“We’ve been working and adapting our approach to meet the moment,” said John Wilson, chief financial officer of Sabey Data Centers, in an interview. “There used to be a time where we would say that our track record could speak for itself, and that’s clearly no longer the case.”

Communities nationwide have raised concerns about the facilities’ effect on rising electric bills, their water use for cooling electronics and the noise they generate.

Broader fears about artificial intelligence are also stoking the pushback, including worries that it will replace workers and appropriate human-created content, and that it can provide erroneous information, foster unhealthy relationships with users, or facilitate scams or terrorist attacks.

A renewed effort to regulate data centers is expected in January in Washington state — home to the headquarters of Sabey, Microsoft and Amazon.

Earlier this year, state Rep. Beth Doglio, D-Olympia, pushed legislation that included ratepayer protections, transparency on environmental impacts and clean energy requirements. Microsoft and the national Data Center Coalition opposed the bill, which failed.

Doglio has been meeting with stakeholders, including tech companies and data center developers, and will try again in the coming legislative session under noticeably different conditions. Fellow lawmakers, she said, will take on AI’s intersection with privacy, education, labor and other issues.

“It’s going to be a very tech-heavy session,” Doglio said.

Before voters weighed in this month, tech companies launched efforts to assuage public fears.

In January, Microsoft released its “Community First AI Infrastructure” initiative, which includes promises to pay the company’s full power costs, reject local property tax breaks, replenish more water than it uses, train local workers and fund AI education and community programs.

Last month, Amazon announced its “Built Together” program, pledging to invest $1 billion over five years in the U.S. communities where it operates data centers, including support for education and civic projects. The initiative offers similar commitments to Microsoft’s on transparency and covering its energy costs.

Wilson said Sabey also works with communities to understand and meet their needs. When a proposed campus in Indianapolis triggered significant pushback, the company brought community members to Quincy, a central Washington data center hub, to tour a Sabey facility and hear from locals about their experience.

“It requires a lot of patience. It requires a lot of transparency in our communication,” Wilson said. Sabey’s Decatur Technology Park in Indianapolis is moving ahead, and the company aims to bring it online in 2030.

But the even bigger challenge now is statewide moratoriums and data center bans — a scale of resistance that is difficult to address even when there is local support.

Oregon Gov. Tina Kotek in October directed a state agency to pause processing and issuing permits for new and expanding data centers until July 2027. Umatilla, where Sabey is developing its first Oregon campus, declared a local state of emergency over the potential loss of investment and revenue, the Oregon Capital Chronicle reported.

Even with the surge in resistance, “we’ve been able to find our way through on every project that we’ve had,” Wilson said. “I’m knocking on wood as I say that.”

Corporate commitments alone may no longer be enough to win public confidence, said Brad Smith, Microsoft vice chair and president, in a GeekWire interview. Communities increasingly want laws requiring all data center developers to build responsibly — a shift he called a “watershed” moment, “but one we anticipated.”

“[P]eople want to see everyone who builds a data center do it in a responsible way,” Smith added, “and they feel that the only way that one can obtain that result is to have some new laws that are put in place.”

Come next year, that could include Washington and impact local tech giants, Sabey and others. Baxtel, a data center industry research firm, lists 29 Microsoft facilities operating in Washington and four under construction, with Sabey operating eight facilities.

Amazon plans to build its first large-scale data centers in Washington, with proposed campuses in Walla Walla County and the Tri-Cities. Amazon did not respond to repeated requests for comment on this story.

Doglio said the next version of her bill will be more stringent. “We have to have a strong bill,” she said, adding that the public and members of her party are calling for it. Microsoft did not publicly specify which provisions triggered its opposition to the previous bill.

Wilson hopes any legislation, in Washington or elsewhere, allows flexibility and recognizes that data centers vary widely in size, impact and application. Sabey provides data center space and infrastructure for customers ranging from major cloud companies to corporations and community organizations.

“Describing all data centers as one thing is just not accurate,” Wilson said, “and it doesn’t understand the realities of the industry itself.”

Smith expressed optimism that U.S. lawmakers can find a middle ground that meets the needs of communities and tech companies.

“There’s plenty of room to draft laws that enable people to go forward and build responsible data centers without chilling the economic contributions these data centers make, especially to local communities,” he said.

The Climate Pledge is a commitment to reach net-zero carbon emissions by 2040, co-founded by Amazon and Global Optimism in 2019.

Seven years later, the community has grown to over 700 signatories across 62 industries and 49 countries and territories, reducing emissions, scaling emerging climate solutions, and addressing challenges across operations and value chains.

Digital Detox Gift Guide: 27 Analog Gift Ideas For Lo-Fi Fun

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

From paint-by-numbers to screen-free storytelling, these picks make downtime feel intentional and joyful.

We started noticing it last year: Everyone seemed to be craving less screen time, less doomscrolling, fewer cheap dopamine hits. And not only has that trend continued in 2026, but it’s gotten even stronger. If anything, we’re all more fried! So analog is having a full-blown moment, which happily makes for some seriously great gifting.

After all, there’s just a special, cozy kind of magic in a night with no screens, isn’t there? A puzzle spread across the dining room table. A record spinning while parents dance in the kitchen (and their kids feign disgust in the background). Coloring and baking and crafting and playing board games — comfortcore activities that keep hands busy and minds engaged.

So this holiday season, give the gift of logging off. From paint-by-numbers to an heirloom-quality chess set, here are our favorite picks for anyone (yourself included) who’s ready to put the phone in a drawer and be fully present for all the holiday merriment.

Turn off the TV and start a puzzle! And thanks to Completing The Puzzle, you no longer have the excuse, “But I don't know what to do with it when I'm done!” They've figured that all out for you. Completing The Puzzle is the first-ever puzzle rental subscription with high-quality puzzles delivered right to your door. Once you finish, pack it up, send it back, and wait for your new one to arrive. — Kate Auletta, Editor in Chief

I have said for about 100 years that I wanted to get into embroidery. Whenever I see something embroidered at an antique mall or thrift store, I am enchanted, but... they are not cheap. Even thrifted! So, why not make my own? Clever Poppy is the perfect way to get an introduction to embroidery if you're a newbie like me. In their kits, they provide step-by-step instructions as well as everything you need to get started. — Katie Garrity, Senior Editor, News & Social

Normalize getting your mom a knit or crochet kit! If they like fiber arts, they will love it. Sometimes people gift me a few balls of yarn, which is amazing, but these kits ensure that I have enough to complete a project. I love Lion Brand's selections, and this cardigan is gorgeous and pretty easy to pull off, too! — Sarah Aswell, Deputy Editor, News & Social

There’s something so deeply satisfying about dropping the needle on a record. You can’t skip songs, you can’t scroll... you actually listen to an album in its purest form. This record player makes that ritual easy, and it looks good doing it with its cool retro-inspired case and little viewing window. It plays both LPs and singles, and it’s compact enough to move wherever you want. I can’t wait to hit the local thrift stores and start building my vinyl collection. Honda Investing $3 Billion to Build New Ohio Factory, Increase Hybrid Production in U.S.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The new plant will be capable of producing 240,000 vehicles a year, and multiple new models are currently under development.

Audi may have recently announced its first-ever U.S. production facility, but at least one Japanese automaker with a head start in that race doesn’t seem keen on giving up any places. On Monday, Honda announced it is investing $3 billion in a new central Ohio factory; the plant, which will produce up to 240,000 vehicles annually, is expected to be online by 2029.

Now, with nine production facilities across the U.S., Honda is no stranger to domestic production, but the new factory plan goes beyond merely adding production capacity. Instead, the company is targeting increased production of hybrid-electric vehicles and their accompanying drivetrain parts. The new plant will sit between Honda’s current East Liberty and Marysville Auto Plants, the carmaker said, and will focus on multiple vehicle types and powertrains, including hybrid and battery-electric vehicles. Honda said the plant will be one of the most advanced factories in the world, leveraging leading-edge automation, data-driven technologies and flexible production systems.

"As Honda approaches 50 years of building products close to our customers in America, we are making one of the most significant commitments in our history to position Honda for the next 50 years,” said Bob Nelson, executive vice president of American Honda Motor Co., Inc. “These investments strengthen America’s role as a cornerstone of Honda’s global future and reflect our confidence in our more than 30,000 Honda associates and the strength of American manufacturing. By expanding our comprehensive R&D and manufacturing capabilities, we will be better positioned to serve our customers and contribute to the economic vitality of the communities where we operate.”

Building another plant in Ohio was an easy choice for Honda. Honda’s U.S. supply base is nearby, and the automaker has a strong reputation among the region’s skilled workforce. The Japanese automaker has previously committed to launching 15 new Honda and Acura hybrid models globally by 2030; North America is the target for many of these models, which are currently in development at R&D facilities in California and Ohio. All told, Honda said the new plant will increase its annual U.S. production capacity to approximately 1.3 million vehicles a year.

The company’s Alabama Auto Plant will also receive new investment to expand engine production for a new V-6 hybrid engine; Honda will retool the engine production facility and one of the production lines to accommodate Passport SUV production. The Anna Engine Plant in Ohio will also be retooled, to produce a next-generation 4-cylinder Atkinson-cycle engine for future compact and midsize vehicles built in North America. Honda Transmission Plant-Ohio will be retooled for two next-generation Honda Hybrid systems in compact vehicles, while Honda Transmission Plant-Georgia will be retooled for next-generation hybrid systems in larger vehicles. All this retooling will account for a 250-percent increase in hybrid powertrain system capacity in Ohio.

“Honda's leadership could have chosen to build this plant anywhere in the world, but they chose Ohio because there are so many advantages to being here,” said Ohio governor Mike DeWine. “For almost half a century, Honda and Ohio have grown together, and generations of families have built their careers on Honda's manufacturing lines. Today's announcement isn't just an investment in a new facility—it's an investment in Ohio's people and Ohio's future.”

A New York transplant hailing from the Pacific Northwest, Emmet White has a passion for anything that goes: cars, bicycles, planes, and motorcycles. After learning to ride at 17, Emmet worked in the motorcycle industry before joining Autoweek in 2022 and Road & Track in 2024. The woes of alternate side parking have kept his fleet moderate, with a 2014 Volkswagen Jetta GLI and a BMW 318i E30 street parked in his Queens community.

American Alliance of Museums Makes Case for Policy Proposals Following Democrats’ Landslide Election Win

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Since President Donald Trump returned to office last year, his administration has made reining in the cultural sphere a core plank of his agenda.

Those efforts have included slashing federal funding for the National Endowment for the Humanities (NEH) and the National Endowment for the Arts (NEA); attempting to remake the John F. Kennedy Center for the Performing Arts in Washington, D.C., in his image; and unleashing a relentless barrage of executive orders, attempted firings, and investigations targeting the Smithsonian Institution and its affiliated museums. The campaign reached a fever pitch in July, when the White House released a 162-page report detailing supposed “ideological capture” at the National Museum of American History. Two congressional hearings followed, in which lawmakers grilled the museum’s director, Anthea Hartig, over her perceived failure to uphold the institution’s mission. Related Articles Nearly Everyone Is Concerned About How Museums Use AI in Exhibitions, Survey Says After a Museum Explores Homes Destroyed by US-Made Munitions, House Republicans Demand Information on Smithsonian's Curatorial Process

The American Alliance of Museums (AAM), a leading industry group for museums, has steadfastly defended cultural institutions facing Trump’s wrath, rebuking the administration’s efforts in public statements.

Now, with the political winds shifting and Democrats having secured their biggest midterm election victory in decades, AAM isn’t wasting time. In a statement released November 4, the day after the election, the organization said it plans to push the newly elected Congress to restore or increase funding for the Institute of Museum and Library Services (IMLS), as well as for the NEA and NEH. (Trump’s proposed 2025 budget would have decimated IMLS, but following an advocacy campaign led by AAM, and legal challenges from the American Library Association and the American Federation of State, County and Municipal Employees, its budget was largely restored.) AAM also called for lifting the House Appropriations Committee’s current ban on earmark funding for museums.

Asked about the organization’s priorities for the incoming Congress, an AAM spokesperson told ARTnews, “We’re focused on continuing to build strong bipartisan support for museums, protecting federal funding for museums, and ensuring museums can fulfill their educational missions without political interference which is an important part of the conversation for the entire field.”

AAM did not offer details on how Congress might prevent political interference in the museum field. In late September, a group of Democratic lawmakers led by Representative Melanie Stansbury of New Mexico introduced a bill to protect the independence of the Smithsonian Institution, a measure AAM endorsed. At the time, Devon Akmon, chair of AAM’s board, said of the bill, “Protecting the Smithsonian’s scholarly independence honors that public trust and affirms a principle fundamental to all museums: scholarship and interpretation must be guided by evidence, expertise, and professional standards, not political influence.” The bill has not advanced since its introduction and will need to be reintroduced when the new Congress convenes in January.

On December 2, AAM will host a live webinar with Brandy Dillingham, its director of advocacy, and Nichole Westin, its senior director of government and field relations, on how the election results may affect museums.

Leftover Fried Chicken Is Juicier With This 2-Step Reheating Method

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Fried chicken is one of those dishes that, if you're making it at home, requires plenty of patience and precision. You must wait for the chicken to fully brine in the marinade, a process that can take as many as eight hours (and up to 24 if you're using the Raising Cane's method). Then, you'll have to make sure the oil is at the proper temperature; if it's too hot, for example, your chicken will likely burn on the outside before it fully cooks on the inside. With all this work going into making perfectly crispy fried chicken, you'll want the leftovers — that is, if you have any! — to sing just as beautifully in the future as the chicken did when it was fresh. And to do that, you might use the two-step reheating method, which involves your microwave and an air fryer.

Your microwave? Really? It's true, microwaves are usually not the most desirable appliances when it comes to reheating fried chicken, but hear us out. You'll just use yours as a sort of brief preheat to start the warming process and take the chill off the meat. Try zapping it for about a minute on high — any longer, and that crispy crust you worked so hard to achieve can become tough and chewy, exactly the opposite of the light, crunchy exterior you're trying to revive.

Then, move those drumsticks, thighs, or breasts to the air fryer for about five minutes at 375 degrees Fahrenheit to re-crisp the breading. The microwave step also keeps you from having to air fry the chicken for quite as long, which means less chance that it will burn or dry out in the second step.

Reheating fried chicken is something of a science, but it's not a difficult discipline. You can help the process along if you take the chicken out of the fridge about half an hour before you intend to heat and eat it (just don't forget about it; it's only safe out of the icebox for up to two hours). This allows the chicken to shed even more of that refrigerator chill so that the microwave and the air fryer can do their jobs even more evenly. And be sure to place something underneath the chicken, like parchment paper, in the air fryer to avoid grease dripping to the bottom and causing smoking.

Another way to help the fried chicken heat more evenly is to consider your microwave's science and arrange the larger pieces in a donut shape around a plate, with the smaller pieces on the inside. The chicken on the outside receives more heat in the minute that it gets zapped, but since those pieces are thicker, they can withstand it better.

Then, when it comes to the air fryer step, it's important that you follow general meat reheating best practices and not crowd the basket. Make sure that every piece has enough space for the air to circulate all around it, with no overlapping pieces that could develop soggy spots.

Mozilla Thunderbird email service edges closer to launch with early access

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Some waitlist members get a first look at Thundermail, a paid alternative to Gmail

The Mozilla-backed Thunderbird project is getting closer to a wider launch of a hosted email service designed to complement its desktop mail app.

Now known as Thundermail (what else?), the service was originally developed under the Thunderbird Pro banner, which encompassed paid email, appointment scheduling, and file-sharing services designed to generate additional revenue for the organization.

The org has been inviting people from its Thundermail waitlist to try the service through an early-access program, and this writer just happened to be among them.

Thundermail offers a web-based email interface and can also be accessed through the Thunderbird desktop app, the Thunderbird mobile app for Android, or other compatible email clients. The Thunderbird apps remain free.

Users get an inbox with up to 15 email addresses and three custom domains, plus 30 GB of mail storage and 60 GB of send storage for Thunderbird Send, an end-to-end encrypted file-sharing and large-attachment service. That’s for starters – the org intends to offer those extra features and services at different price points.

Yes, Thundermail is a paid service, which may put off some users who are used to free email accounts offered via Gmail, Outlook.com and Proton Mail, but with the first two of those you do get served ads along with your messages.

The sign-up screens for Thundermail suggest the basic service will cost $6 per month, billed annually. This is roughly equivalent to the £55 we paid for a year on the early access program.

We asked Thunderbird when we can expect to see Thundermail officially launched and open to all comers. We will update if we get an answer. It previously said that a full launch is expected sometime in 2026.

The web-based user interface of Thundermail presents a fairly clean and uncluttered look, and could be likened to Gmail with the screen partitioned into a pane with folders on the left and a larger one to the right displaying messages.

A welcome screen introduces you to the features of Thundermail, which include the ability to work on several drafts at once, schedule delivery times for emails, create folders to arrange messages, contact lists and identities – all pretty much standard for an email system.

We haven’t really had the time to test out Thundermail a great deal since signing up, but there are no great surprises so far, and everything works pretty much the way you would expect it to.

Firefox and the Thunderbird mail client both recently moved to a fortnightly release schedule in line with Mozilla’s plans, and Thunderbird itself gained support for Microsoft’s Exchange late last year. ®

I tested the Google Pixel Tag and it’s the most findable tracker for Android phones — plus it does something Apple AirTag can't

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Pixel Tag delivers an outstanding tracking experience for Android users, matching the AirTag’s $29 price point while beating it out with superior Precision Finding range and persistent alerts when the batter level gets low.

Why you can trust Tom's Guide Our writers and editors spend hours analyzing and reviewing products, services, and apps to help find what's best for you. Find out more about how we test, analyze, and rate.

Over the past few years, I’ve tested my fair share of Bluetooth trackers — ranging from wallet-thin cards like the Statik SmartCard Incognito to the Pebblebee Halo, which can potentially help elderly individuals in emergency situations. Google has expanded its Find Hub network, finally giving Android users plenty of options, but many of these trackers share one glaring limitation: pinpoint proximity tracking.

While iPhone users have long enjoyed the superb performance of Precision Finding — leaning on Ultra Wideband (UWB) for pinpoint accuracy — most Android trackers don’t have that luxury. Instead, they rely on basic Bluetooth proximity that only shows broad geographic circles on a map.

Google’s Pixel Tag is one of the few Android trackers that packages Ultra Wideband (UWB) and Bluetooth Channel Sounding directly into Google’s native ecosystem. As a result, the Pixel Tag delivers true visual Precision Finding to Android devices. After putting it to the test, I found it holds one distinct advantage over the AirTag and one of the best Bluetooth trackers you can get.

Ultra-Wideband (UWB) + Bluetooth Channel Sounding

Easily the best feature of the Pixel Tag is its ability to lock down an exact position with outstanding Precision Finding range, which actually managed to outperform even Apple's AirTag 2 in my testing. Using the Find My Device app on an Android phone, you get intuitive on-screen visual guidance, complete with compass-like directional arrows and real-time distance readouts that point you right where you need to go.

While the AirTag 2 tapped out around 74 feet in my testing before dropping its Ultra Wideband connection, the Pixel Tag held on slightly farther, reaching roughly 82 feet. That extra reach eliminates the guesswork of tracking down misplaced gear, like my keys when I've completely forgotten where I left them. Plus, you can still trigger a loud audio chime if the tag slips deep between couch cushions and you can't spot it right away.

The AirTag has an unmistakable look, but Google has delivered an equally compelling design — especially when it comes to size. At just 5.4mm thick, the Pixel Tag is significantly sleeker than the AirTag, making it far less conspicuous when slipped into a standard wallet. With its minimalist, matte-finished polycarbonate body, I really dig the pebble-like aesthetic, which does a far better job of masking smudges and fingerprints than the AirTag's scratch-prone stainless steel back.

Even with that slimmed-down profile, Google didn’t cut corners on durability, outfitting it with a rugged IP67 dust- and water-resistance rating. Plus, swapping out the CR2032 coin-cell battery is painless — all it takes is a quick twist of the back cover.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Beyond tracking down my keys, wallet, backpack, or other gear, the Pixel Tag offers another standout feature you won’t find on the AirTag: the ability to ring your phone. With a simple double-press of the physical button on the front, you can instantly trigger your phone to ring at full volume — even if it's set to silent or vibrate. This reverse-finding capability is a lifesaver in a crunch when you're rushing out the door and can't find your phone anywhere.

One of the biggest annoyances I have with the AirTag is discovering weeks or even months later that it's dead. Sure, my iPhone might have sent a low-battery notification, but those alerts are easy to forget. That’s why I’m stoked about the Pixel Tag’s persistent automated reminders, which offer huge peace of mind if I overlook that initial heads-up.

Google rates the user-replaceable CR2032 coin-cell battery for up to a full year of typical use. While I haven’t had the Pixel Tag long enough to drain it down and trigger an alert firsthand, knowing Find Hub will send a notification “every 2 weeks until it’s replaced” ensures it won't quietly drop off my radar. Usually, I’m not a fan of that level of notification nagging, but in this case, it's a welcome safety net.

Despite having some key advantages over the AirTag, there’s one area where the Pixel Tag falls behind its rival: accessory availability. You can find an AirTag mount, leather loop, rugged clip, or pet collar attachment with a quick search online. The Pixel Tag, however, launches with very few options.

In fact, Google doesn't even offer any official accessories right out of the gate. The only ones I’ve been able to track down so far are basic silicone cases from Walmart. Because the Pixel Tag’s design doesn't include a built-in lanyard hole or keyring cutout, you'll need a dedicated case or adhesive mount just to attach it to your keys or backpack. For early adopters, finding the exact style or mount you want right now will require some patience while third-party makers ramp up production.

Considering how long the Pixel line has been around, you’d think Google would’ve come out with its own AirTag rival sooner. Even so, the Pixel Tag is a long-overdue addition — and it’s easily one of the most capable and well-rounded Bluetooth trackers on the market.

Between its class-leading Precision Finding range, compact design, and reverse-phone-ringing feature, it addresses the pinpoint tracking compromises that have long plagued the Android ecosystem. The only other real alternatives with Ultra Wideband support are Motorola’s Moto Tag and Samsung’s SmartTag3. You still get all the essential safeguards expected from a flagship tracker, too, including left-behind alerts and a dedicated Lost Mode that allows anyone who finds your gear to scan it and get in touch.

At $29 for a single one (or $99 for a four-pack), Google matches Apple dollar for dollar — but delivers greater range and features the AirTag simply can’t match.

John’s a senior editor covering phones for Tom’s Guide. He’s no stranger in this area having covered mobile phones and gadgets since 2008 when he started his career. Ignore Wrong-Number Texts – Many Crypto Investment Scams Start With a Reply

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

A friendly text meant for someone else is a common opening for crypto investment scams, and a polite reply is exactly what the scammer wants. Don’t answer, even to say it’s the wrong number. If someone you met by text ever brings up investing, stop replying and never invest where they tell you to.

Picture a text from an unknown number that reads, “Hi Jessica, I confirmed our dinner reservation for Friday at 7!” Pointing out the mistake feels like simple courtesy, but to a scammer, your reply proves a friendly person is on the other end.

The FBI and the Federal Trade Commission describe what comes next. The stranger apologizes and keeps the chat going until it becomes a friendship or a romance. Then they mention how well they’re doing trading crypto and offer to show you how. Early on, the trading site they steer you to shows fast gains and often lets you withdraw some, which makes it look real. When you try to cash out everything, the account freezes and the site says you owe “taxes” or fees to unlock it.

Crypto investment fraud was the biggest source of losses reported to the FBI in 2025, at $7.2 billion. When the FBI contacted 3,780 victims that year to warn them, 78% didn’t know they were being scammed.

Instead of replying, forward the text to 7726 (SPAM), which helps your carrier block similar messages, then delete it.

If you’ve already replied, these signs give the scam away:

How long you’ve been talking doesn’t matter. Never put money into an investment that someone you met online introduced.

If you’ve already sent money, stop. Don’t pay a tax or release fee to free your balance, since paying never unlocks it. Report the scam at ic3.gov, the FBI’s Internet Crime Complaint Center, with every transaction detail you have.

Then expect a second scam. The FBI says almost every victim later hears from a fake lawyer, recovery firm, or law enforcement officer who offers to get the money back for a fee. The FBI never asks victims for money, so treat any recovery offer that comes with a fee as the next scam.

That compensation may influence which products we cover and where and how they appear on a page, including the order in which they appear. We do not include every company or offer available in the marketplace.

STAT+: Former Eli Lilly rep claims she was fired for complaining about marketing of Mounjaro diabetes drug

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Ed’s stories explore prescription drug pricing, affordability and access, as well issues surrounding patents, litigation, and legislation. He is also the author of the morning Pharmalittle newsletter and the afternoon Pharmalot newsletter.

A former Eli Lilly sales rep filed a lawsuit alleging that she was fired after complaining the company was promoting its Mounjaro diabetes drug to combat weight loss before receiving regulatory approval to do so.

Jennifer Mansion, who was hired as a senior territory manager in Dallas four years ago, claimed that she observed other Lilly sales reps promoting the diabetes drug as a salve for obesity. Mansion also contended that a physician touted Mounjaro as a weight loss option during a luncheon sponsored by the drugmaker, according to the suit filed in federal court in Texas.

Such promotions are known as off-label marketing in the pharmaceutical industry. The phrase refers to promoting a medicine for a usage that was not approved by the Food and Drug Administration. A physician may prescribe a medicine for an unapproved use, but a drugmaker is not permitted to market its drug for that purpose.

Gold Bull Peter Schiff Sees Bitcoin Crashing Toward Cycle Lows Near $59,500

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

American stockbroker Peter Schiff has warned of an imminent Bitcoin crash following the 2026 U.S. midterm elections.

Speaking to his followers on X, the well-known economic and capital markets analyst warned of a potential Bitcoin plunge.

His remarks suggested that BTC is set to lose resilience due to recent events that have eroded investor confidence and pushed crypto prices lower.

Based on his market observation, the analyst noted that since the recently concluded 2026 midterm elections, U.S. stock markets have surged around 12% while Bitcoin dropped by 4.6%. Over the same period, gold and silver prices have risen by 5% and 7%, respectively.

According to Peter Schiff, price hikes in equities, gold, and silver indicate that Wall Street and traditional investors are celebrating the election outcome, marked by Democrats winning control of both the House and Senate.

Initially, investors feared a lengthy political battle after the Democratic sweep. However, traditional capital assets rebounded after Trump accepted the outcome, dispelling fears of an election dispute. In short, investors expect a divided Congress to clamp down on government spending and borrowing.

Also, the yield on the benchmark 10-year Treasury note dropped by 4.91% following the Democratic victory. This means lower Treasury yields support stock valuations by reducing borrowing costs and eroding bond returns, making them less attractive relative to equities and precious metals. This explains strong gains in U.S. stocks and rare metals.

Bitcoin weakened after the midterm election, noting that the timing indicates that the vote and the subsequent Binance hack impacted investor sentiment. Today, BTC trades at $73,135, after losing a crucial support at $75,000 and falling from its recent $80,000 peak.

Schiff is aware that Bitcoin always falls after midterm elections. He knows more drops are coming now that the asset has lost its key support at $75k. He believes that the cryptocurrency is primed to crash towards its cycle bottom (around $59,517), urging investors to sell before the short window closes.

Rubix Battery: Storage Designed for Solar Installers

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

By James Mast, Managing Partner, Rubix Battery

AI and data center growth, extreme weather, and baseload retirement are straining the US electric grid. For homeowners and businesses, reliable access to electricity is increasingly important. That need is driving solar battery adoption. Solar power with battery storage can help keep systems running when grid power is unavailable and give property owners greater control over energy.

Energy storage is becoming an increasingly important part of residential, commercial, and off-grid power systems. In fact, the off-grid power systems market is expected to increase to USD 7.35 billion by the year 2032, while growing at a Compounded Annual Growth Rate (CAGR) of 15.0%. For solar contractors, that growth brings new opportunities, but they will need battery solutions that accommodate different project requirements without adding needless complexity. As more customers consider storage, solar installers need battery solutions that can be configured for different loads, applications, and long-term goals.

Rubix Battery is a U.S.-based energy storage manufacturer specializing in lithium iron phosphate (LiFePO4) solar batteries. Drawing on personal, hands-on experience with solar and off-grid installations, the company develops battery systems with an emphasis on practical installation, scalable capacity, and long-term performance.

Battery specifications are important, but they’re only one part of what makes a battery good for a project. Installation requirements, wiring access, inverter compatibility, and the ability to expand a system all affect how well a product fits.

Rubix Battery knows what makes installation easier because the batteries were designed by installers with hands-on, real-world experience. They bring that knowledge and experience into product design. Features like enclosed wiring compartments, magnetic covers, modular stacking, and closed-loop communication with compatible inverters help simplify system setup. Integrated safety components and designs that eliminate the need for external racking or exposed cabling further streamline installation.

This installer-focused approach also gives contractors flexibility when planning systems for customers with different energy demands. No matter what type of project, both residential and off-grid, Rubix offers two battery platforms to address a range of storage needs while giving customers reliable energy storage that grows as their needs do.

Battery performance over time depends in part on how effectively individual cells remain balanced. Rubix Battery uses Impactiv Balancing™ technology in every battery to help maintain consistent voltage throughout the battery pack.

Unlike traditional passive balancing, which dissipates excess energy from higher-voltage cells as heat, Impactiv Balancing™ transfers energy from higher-voltage cells to lower-voltage cells. The system also incorporates dissipative balancing when a cell reaches a critically high voltage.

With Impactiv Balancing™, Rubix batteries balance cells at up to 20 times the speed of passive balancing and can add up to 30% more battery life. Effective cell balancing is an important consideration when evaluating battery capacity, performance, and long-term reliability for installers and system owners.

Rubix Battery offers two primary product lines: the modular Stack Series and the high-capacity R-Series. Both use LiFePO4 chemistry and include Impactiv Balancing™ technology, closed-loop communication with compatible inverters, and a 10-year warranty.

Rubix Stack Series solar batteries are modular LiFePO4 energy storage systems designed for residential and light commercial applications. Each Rubix Stack Series battery module delivers 5.12kWh of energy storage, scalable up to 32 batteries in parallel for a maximum system capacity of 163.84kWh.

Rubix R-Series solar batteries provide higher-capacity storage for demanding applications, including off-grid, agricultural, and larger energy systems. Each Rubix R-Series battery module delivers 14.336kWh to 16kWh of energy storage across three configurations (24V/560Ah, 48V/280Ah, and 48V/314Ah) and is scalable up to 63 batteries in parallel for a maximum system capacity of 1,008kWh.

Selecting a battery platform involves balancing capacity, installation requirements, and a customer’s long-term energy needs. Rubix Battery’s Stack Series and R-Series give installers two approaches to system design, from modular residential storage to high-capacity off-grid applications.

With LiFePO4 chemistry, Impactiv Balancing™ technology, and features designed around installation realities, Rubix Battery offers battery options for contractors working on a range of solar projects.

Explore the product specifications and connect with the team at Rubix Battery to learn more about its energy storage solutions.

Jobe Bellingham described the prospect of representing England alongside his brother Jude as a moment beyond words.

The midfielder earned his maiden call-up to Thomas Tuchel’s squad following impressive performances for England Under-21s and Borussia Dortmund.

If he features in upcoming Nations League matches against Croatia and Spain, they will become the 22nd set of brothers to play for England.

"I was, as you can imagine, ecstatic and so excited and then when I took the call later on, it was just quite surreal. You don’t really know what to do with yourself in that moment, but you’re just so pleased and I couldn’t stop smiling.

"I feel really, really proud. It was something that I’d hoped for and I felt like I was making it a little bit more possible each week with each game so yeah, I’m really pleased."

Polestar Formula 2030 Concept Makes Us Hope Polestar Will Be Back In America By 2030

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Chinese-owned Polestar was banned from selling cars in the U.S. back in June, and like a scorned ex showing you what you're missing after a breakup, it's now revealed one of the coolest designs in recent memory: the Formula 2030 concept car. Look at what they took from us.

It's got a low, wide, shooting brake shape. The big front door, little rear door setup is pure enthusiast bait, and the switchgear inside looks like it came straight off of professional audio equipment. The Formula 2030 isn't just a for-show concept car, either, because Polestar says it will indeed "point towards future design direction for the brand."

Specifically, "various design attributes" seen here will actually show up in the next-gen Polestar 2 and upcoming Polestar 7 compact SUV. The Formula 2030 is billed as a compact four-door sports car, not really fitting neatly into any existing segment. It sits lower than the Polestar 5 grand tourer, fits between the 2 compact sedan and 7 size-wise, and is probably closest to the Polestar 4 in spirit.

It already looks great at a glance, but things get way more interesting once you start opening stuff up. Both the hood and rear hatch open up clamshell-style, exposing the natural fiber composite chassis underneath and orange Öhlins dampers. The half-size rear doors are coach doors, giving the Formula no B-pillar and a huge opening when all doors are open.

Inside, a very curved instrument screen measuring 20 inches sits on top of the steering column while a 12.2-inch center touchscreen sits in the middle. That touchscreen can physically lower when the driver switches drive modes to Performance, and raise when the car is in Autonomous. Below this screen is an array of up to eight customizable rocker switches and knobs. You can swap the specific switches or knobs out, like you can with, say, a pro-grade video game controller, and their individual functions can also be configured to your liking. All four bucket seats sit low in the cabin and get inflatable bolsters and cushions for better support.

I've never really thought about how rare four-spoke wheels are, but looking at the 22s on this Polestar concept, I'm simultaneously sad that they aren't more common but also completely understand why that's the case. They're definitely weird, but I also sort of dig it?

It all adds up to a funky yet beautiful design that makes us hope Polestar will return to the U.S. market by the Formula 2030's namesake. Given the political conditions that led to its banning and how those tides are turning in this country, that scenario actually feels like a real possibility.

“F*** Keywords” – Well, What Are You Replacing Them With? via @sejournal, @chrisgreenseo

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Declaring keywords dead is easy. Explaining what you’ll track, how you’ll govern content, and what stakeholders buy into is the harder part.

Every so often, someone in SEO announces “f*** keywords” or a similar sentiment. It’s not really a “hot take.” Moreover, it’s something that we see time and again.

Sometimes it’s a fair reaction to how reluctant some people in the industry are to change. Other times it feels more like a meme response, or a flex. A way to show you’ve moved on from old-school SEO without saying much about what you do differently, exaggerating a tension between pro/anti keywords without understanding it.

I have some sympathy for the sentiment for sure. I’ve worked on too many campaigns where success came down to whether a handful of keywords went up or down. Once those rankings became THE GOAL, it was easy to spend more time explaining two dropped places than asking whether we met the primary business need. More damagingly, in the worst cases we were left making decisions just for promoting keywords, rather than the business goals they were supposed to enable.

I don’t know how many people still run campaigns that way, and I suspect fewer than the “f*** keywords” cry implies. What interests me is how quickly the argument goes from “this is a bad way to judge a campaign” to “why are we thinking about keywords at all?”

Take an example search like [hotels near Heathrow with a shuttle]. It’s a decent shorthand for what someone wants to know before they book. Is there a shuttle to the terminal? Does it run when my flight lands? Do I need to reserve a seat? It also gives you a clue about what language you need to use to gain recognition from the searcher.

If guests are looking for a shuttle, the page titled “ground transportation solutions” is making them do more work than they need to. Again, a fictitious example, but I have lost track of how many times in my career I’ve seen businesses wanting to target the ways they define their products/services, NOT how people search for them.

In this instance, what is more important is that the initial search query becomes emblematic of a series of questions that need answering, potentially across a series of pages. Whether we call this a hub and spoke model, or a content cluster, whatever, we have captured part of the customer journey with a query and then determined the potential related queries.

This is where a keyword map (or site map) earns its keep, long after the initial research is done and the content written and published. Imagine that six months later someone rewrites a particular location page. The shuttle timetable moves into a general FAQ and a new transfers page goes live. All three changes are totally logical, but taken together, they can leave the site without a clear place to answer the question the location page used to own. Without realizing it, you have sleepwalked into undoing the research you have done before.

If you’ve recorded which page serves that need, the next content editor or SEO has something to go back to. They can ensure that intent/search query is still considered, move content responsibility, update the internal links, and ensure the redirects are done. The keyword map preserves the reasoning behind those original decisions and helps you tell the difference between an intentional change and targeting that drifted over a few rounds of perfectly reasonable edits.

The keywords aren’t the single truth, nor are they to be treated as an immutable dogma, but throwing them in the “f***it bucket” could be missing something important.

Too cool for keywords now? Great, but how does governance work? How do you ensure you’re not wasting the money already spent on research? Yeah, I know – that sounds tedious, because it can be. And building a process to make sure this isn’t painfully manual IS hard.

You can organize your work around topics, and you can use embeddings to help group the language – in fact you should! But at some point, you’ll at least need to benchmark overall visibility, and you still have to choose what to type into a search box or put in a prompt. You can aggregate your rank/prompt tracking for a content cluster – which is needed – but before the aggregation comes selections of individual prompts and terms.

Stakeholders need to buy into those prompts/terms/queries that make up that group OR you 10x the number of specific tracked elements to totally de-emphasize any individual one. Not a bad idea, but you increase your costs significantly and still need to consider the wording, terms and intents on individual pages when it comes to making optimizations.

Instead, I’d happily track a few representative searches for a given page, where I would also want to see which URL(s) appeared and whether they answered the question. Those observations can tell us whether the page is doing some of the work we intended, whether another page is potentially conflicting and where our own content is falling behind competitors.

“Wait, we still use keywords in our data, but we don’t report on the success of individual keywords anymore!” That is perfectly sensible, but it turns out that maybe, perhaps, the “f*** keywords” position is more clickbait than actual smart thinking – right?

These are SEO basics; I totally understand. Prompt tracking and AI search do ultimately de-emphasize the importance of exact keywords in favor of meaning. But consider that embedding text into vectors is so “machines” can understand it. Your team, colleagues and stakeholders still need the human version to understand how to optimize and tell the story of what is/isn’t working.

All of this helps us understand what we’re visible for, but it doesn’t tell us what happened next – and that is perhaps the biggest weakness.

In Search City, I wrote about the temptation to only optimize for the bits of the search journey we can see. Rankings were always one of the easier bits to observe – that is why we have had an almighty job replacing them with a more useful metric, and why the issues hitting prompt trackers are so disruptive to the industry! But now, even when someone makes a search and sees you for that query/keyword/prompt, the part that links that moment to a click and then an important business event can be almost totally disconnected.

Knowing whether we “rank” for a keyword or not is still an important data point. Fixating on that single data point is mad – but that goes for any single data point. We need to use it to build the picture, build a story and provide insight.

If I went back to any of those campaigns I ran where I felt single terms were prioritized (to a fault), I’d probably still keep some of the same keywords – as triggering as that may be.

I’d use them to agree which searches mattered the most, decide which pages should serve them and check whether those pages still do the job. When it came time to judge the campaign, I’d want to know what happened to the customers/clicks we were trying to win. That was the question the rankings were supposed to help us answer in the first place, and it is still something they can help answer today.

This post was originally published on Chris Green SEO.

The RAM crisis has caused a spike in the price of PCs, and that's made 2026 a difficult year for creatives and gamers alike. This is an entry-level gaming PC with a Intel Core 5 processor, Nvidia GeForce RTX 5060 8GB graphics, 16GB RAM and a 512GB SSD. It would struggle with editing heavy effects on high-resolution footage in a program like DaVinci Resolve, but the dedicated graphics will have you covered for photo editing and lighter video editing.

Whether it's for gaming or content creation, there are two big advantages of a desktop PC over a laptop. Firstly, you tend to get more bang for your buck in the first place. As a rule, you tend to get more powerful internals, in the form of a superior CPU and GPU and more RAM, compared to what you would get in a laptop at the same price point (of course, some of this is outweighed by the fact that you also need to invest in a monitor and peripherals).

But the biggest advantage of a desktop PC is upgradability. If you find you outgrow the RTX 5060 too quickly, or processes are getting bottlenecked and you need more RAM, or more storage, you can buy the additional internals that you need as you need them and upgrade the configuration yourself, potentially multiple times.

Bring Up The Bodies, often regarded as Hilary Mantel's masterpiece (more of a Place Of Greater Safety man myself), opens thus: "His children are falling from the sky. He watches from horse-back, acres of England stretching behind him; they drop, gilt-winged, each with a blood-filled gaze".

This is a description of Thomas Cromwell's falcons in 1535. It is also a description of the life of Franklin Clinton in 2026. Los Santos is awash in bodies because I have installed PeopleFalling – BloodSky, a mod which sends an endless wave of people plummeting to Earth like a horrible hard rain. "A modification called 'People Falling' or as I call it, 'Blood Sky'", writes the author, confusingly, as they presumably chose both names.

"When you press the 'X' key, characters drop from the sky. You can change the activation button in the .ini file". This is a lie. It's actually the B key. My relationship with PeopleFalling AKA BloodSky got off to a rocky start.

It does work though. Sort of. Tap B (not X) and the rainy season begins—people fall silently to the ground in a kind of reverse-Rapture situation, absolutely all of them physics-free and adopting the same stomach-sleeper position when they finally make contact with sidewalk, with highway, with rooftop.

Then it ends, and no matter how much you tap B—notifications reading "Falling people Active" and "Falling People Deactive" flashing in the lower right of your screen—they never come back. It's like you have witnessed a beautiful, once-in-a-lifetime miracle, like a full solar eclipse or Halley's comet.

Also, when the process is ongoing it really alarms my bodyguard, who responds like a dog hearing a bang in the house at night.

Git is releasing updates addressing six security vulnerabilities affecting recursive clones, reference handling, merges, binary patches, and imports. Here is what users need to know.

Today, the Git project is releasing new versions to address six security vulnerabilities. These include arbitrary code execution when recursively cloning a malicious repository, writes to unintended files, and memory corruption when processing merges or binary patches.

The fixes are included in Git 2.56.1, with backports to Git 2.55.1, 2.54.1, 2.53.1, 2.52.1, 2.51.3, and 2.50.2. We recommend updating to Git 2.56.1 or the patched release for your maintenance series, and updating any applications that bundle Git.

Git checks paths before writing files into a working tree, including rejecting paths that would overwrite its own repository metadata. However, an integer truncation in Git’s index handling can cause an extremely long path to pass validation under one name and then be written using a shorter name.

An attacker can use this discrepancy to replace a submodule’s .git file with a symbolic link pointing to a directory containing malicious configuration or hooks. When Git subsequently uses that directory, it can execute attacker-controlled code during a recursive clone.

The fix rejects extremely long index entry paths before their lengths can be truncated.

When cloning an empty repository using Git protocol v2, the server can advertise a HEAD symref pointing to an unborn branch (one that has no commits yet). Git did not validate the symref’s target before storing it locally.

A malicious server can supply a name containing path-traversal components. With the files reference backend, a later operation that updates the branch can write outside the intended reference directory.

Git now validates the advertised target before accepting it. The release also validates other reference names and symbolic-reference targets received through protocol v2.

When stripping GPG signatures from a message, Git assumed there would be at most two signatures. A specially crafted message containing more than two signatures can cause Git to write past the end of a fixed-size stack array.

This can be triggered by merging a tag containing more than two signatures, causing memory corruption and a potential denial of service. Hosting services that perform these merges on behalf of users can also be affected.

The fix removes the fixed-size array and handles messages with any number of signatures.

Git rejects patches larger than approximately 1 GiB because its patch application code cannot safely handle larger inputs. However, this check only considered the size of the patch as supplied.

Binary patches are compressed and encoded, so a relatively small patch can expand beyond that limit. A crafted patch can bypass the input-size check and cause an out-of-bounds read, crashing Git.

Git now also checks the declared uncompressed size before processing a binary patch.

Git’s merge machinery calculates how much memory it needs for a merge result before writing the output. That calculation can overflow when the result is very large, including when many conflicts are combined with a large conflict-marker-size attribute.

The overflow can cause Git to allocate a buffer that is too small, then write outside it, corrupting heap memory. This affects applications and hosting services that use Git to merge untrusted content, as well as local merges.

The fix uses size-appropriate types and checked arithmetic, rejecting merges whose output size cannot be represented safely.

git fast-import reads a stream of commands to build a repository. Some commands can access files outside the repository, so Git already restricts them when they appear in an untrusted input stream.

Three additional features lacked that protection. The rewrite-submodules-from and rewrite-submodules-to features can read arbitrary files, potentially exposing their contents through error messages. The export-pack-edges option can create files or append data to existing files.

These features are now rejected when supplied through the input stream unless the caller explicitly enables --allow-unsafe-features. Only enable that option when you trust the program generating the stream.

Alongside these fixes, the release adds overflow checks to related index entry allocations during checkout and tree processing. These checks complement the path-length limits above, preventing allocation sizes from being truncated or overflowing before Git copies a path into memory.

Updating is the most effective way to protect against these vulnerabilities. Install a patched version from your operating system or Git distributor, and update applications that include their own copy of Git. Administrators should also update Git installations used by automation and services that process repositories on behalf of users.

If you cannot update immediately, clone only from trusted servers and avoid recursive clones of untrusted repositories. Avoid merging untrusted content or tags, or processing binary patches or fast-import streams from untrusted sources. These precautions do not replace installing the fixes.

Elijah Newren is a Staff Software Engineer at GitHub. He is the designer and implementer of merge-ort, Git's default merge strategy since 2.34, author of git-filter-repo—the history rewriting tool the Git project officially recommends—and a driving force behind sparse-checkout.
